after-save hooks), so the file only ever holds the keywords. With
`mode = "materialized"` every transclusion is inserted when a buffer is
opened (org-transclusion-add-all-on-activate); `mode = false` shows
nothing until you ask.

Transclusions are drawn when an org buffer is shown, redrawn when the
buffer or a source buffer changes (`live`) or a source is written, and,
for source files that aren't loaded, when they change on disk (`watch`,
only while a buffer showing them is loaded).

Editing the source ~
`transclusion_edit` (`<CR>` on a keyword or inserted text, `edit_key`)
opens the source lines in a float (org-transclusion-live-sync-start): the
whole subtree with its drawers, or the lines of a code file with its
filetype. `:w` writes them back into the source: into its buffer when it
is loaded (and writes that buffer when it had no other unsaved changes),
else into the file. Every transclusion of the source is then redrawn and
inserted copies are updated. If the source changed meanwhile so the text
can't be found, nothing is written. `q` closes the float. Elsewhere `<CR>`
keeps its meaning.

Actions and keys (in org buffers) ~
  `<prefix>ua`  transclusion_add            insert the text at point
  `<prefix>uA`  transclusion_add_all        insert every transclusion
  `<prefix>ud`  transclusion_remove         take out the inserted text; on
                                          a virtual one, hide it
  `<prefix>uD`  transclusion_remove_all     take out every inserted text
  `<prefix>ug`  transclusion_refresh        re-read the sources, show
                                          hidden ones again
  `<prefix>ue`  transclusion_edit           edit the source (also `<CR>`)
  `<prefix>uo`  transclusion_open_source    visit the source (`open_source`)
  `<prefix>ut`  transclusion_toggle         virtual transclusions on/off
  `<prefix>uc`  transclusion_detach         replace the transclusion by a
                                          copy of its text
  `<prefix>ul`  transclusion_make_from_link add a `#+transclude:` for the
                                          link at point
  `<prefix>u<`  transclusion_promote        `:level` one up
  `<prefix>u>`  transclusion_demote         `:level` one down
`:Org transclusion_insert [[link]] :level 2` adds a keyword below the
cursor.

Export ~
`#+transclude:` keywords are expanded when a buffer is exported, before
`#+INCLUDE:` keywords, except `:disable-auto` ones (`export = false` turns
this off). Inserted text is not exported twice.

Options ~
  `mode`                   "virtual", "materialized" or false ("virtual")
  `exclude_elements`       element types always left out
                         ({ "property-drawer" })
  `include_first_section`  keep the text before the first headline of a
                         whole file (true)
  `nested`                 expand keywords in transcluded text (true)
  `max_depth`              nesting limit (5)
  `max_virtual_lines`      longest virtual transclusion (400)
  `border`                 left border of virtual lines ("│ ")
  `sign`                   sign on inserted lines, false for none ("▎")
  `show_source`            source at the end of the keyword line (true)
  `live`                   redraw as source buffers change (true)
  `watch`                  watch unloaded source files (true)
  `debounce`               milliseconds before redrawing (150)
  `export`                 expand keywords on export (true)
  `edit_key`               key editing the transclusion at point ("<CR>")
  `edit`                   { window = "float" or an Ex command such as
                         "split", width, height, border }
  `open_source`            "edit", "split", "vsplit" or "tab" ("split")

Highlights: `OrgTransclusionBorder`, `OrgTransclusionSource`,
`OrgTransclusionSign`, `OrgTransclusionError`.

Differences from Emacs org-transclusion ~
  - Virtual lines are the default; Emacs always inserts the text.
  - Inserted text keeps its keyword line above it (Emacs hides the
    keyword while the text is shown).
  - Editing happens in a float written with `:w`, not in an overlay kept
    in sync as you type (org-transclusion-live-sync-start).
  - Nested keywords are expanded as the text is drawn; Emacs expands them
    when you add them.
  - `:thing-at-point` and noweb chunks are not supported, and `:end` is a
    text search (or `/regexp/`), not any Org search option.
  - org-roam doesn't index links in `#+transclude:` lines (like org-roam),
    and inserted text is never in the file, so it isn't indexed either.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Views: kanban, timeline, heatmap, sidebar               *org-extensions-views*

Four visual views of your tasks, each an extension of its own. They share
their plumbing (`lua/org/extensions/views_util.lua`): where entries come
from, filters, the window, jumping back to a heading and redrawing when org
files are written or a TODO state or clock changes. Their global keys live
under `<prefix>V`.

Where a view takes a `source`, it is one of:
  `"agenda"`   the agenda files (default)
  `"buffer"`   the current buffer
  `"subtree"`  the subtree at the cursor (followed while lines are added
             above it)
  a path or glob, or a list of them

A filter (`/` in the kanban board, or words after `:Org kanban`) is an
org-ql sexp when it starts with `(` (|org-extensions-ql|; the query
language is loaded on demand, the ql extension need not be enabled), and a
tags / property match like `work+urgent-home` otherwise (|org-match-syntax|).
The `query` option takes any org-ql query, plain syntax included.

COMMENT and ARCHIVE subtrees are left out, as in the agenda. The views are
scratch buffers; <CR> opens the heading in the window the view was opened
from (floats and tabs close first).

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Kanban board                                           *org-extensions-kanban*

A board with a column per TODO keyword and a card per heading:
>lua
    require("org").setup({ extensions = { kanban = {
      columns = {
        "TODO",
        { "NEXT", "WAITING", name = "Doing", wip = 3 },
        "DONE",
      },
    } } })
<
`:Org kanban [agenda|buffer|subtree|<file>] [filter]` opens it; the actions
`kanban_open` (`<prefix>Vk`), `kanban_buffer` and `kanban_subtree` open it
on the `source` option, the current buffer and the subtree at the cursor.

Columns are the TODO keywords in the order they are defined (with the
keywords of `#+TODO` lines in the source files after them) unless `columns`
lists them; a column can gather several keywords under a `name`. The header shows the
number of cards, or `count/limit` with a WIP limit (`wip` in the column or
the `wip` option), in the error colour with a `!` once over it. Headers use
the TODO keyword faces (`ui.todo_keyword_faces`).

A card shows the priority, the title (wrapped to `title_lines`), the
deadline countdown (◆ in 3d, in the warning colour within the deadline's
warning period, the error colour when overdue), the Effort (◷ 2:30) and the
tags; `card` turns each part on or off (`scheduled` and `category` are off
by default). Cards are sorted by `sort`.

Keys in the board ~
  h / l     move the card to the previous / next column: the heading gets
            the column's first keyword through the regular TODO code, so
            logging, CLOSED, blocking and repeaters work as with `C-c C-t`.
            Columns whose keywords the card's file doesn't have (another
            `#+TODO` sequence) are skipped. With `choose_keyword`, a column
            of several keywords asks which one.
  j / k     next / previous card
  J / K     move the card after the next / before the previous card of the
            column: its subtree moves past the other one in the file. Only
            for cards under the same heading, with `sort = "file"` (in the
            other orders the board would put it back)
  H / L     previous / next column
  <CR>      open the heading
  /         filter (empty clears it)
  r         refresh
  q  <Esc>  quit

The cursor can also be moved onto a card with any motion or the mouse. The
board redraws itself when an org file is written, when one of its files is
changed (in Normal mode; a burst of changes redraws once), and when a TODO
state or clock changes; a board in a hidden tab waits until it is shown.
Moving the selection redraws nothing. A board of thousands of cards takes a
noticeable moment to build (about 0.2 s for 5,000 cards). Moved cards are saved with `save = true` (default:
`agenda.save_after_edit`).

Options (defaults) ~
  `columns`            {} (one per keyword)
  `choose_keyword`     false
  `wip`                {} (limits by column name or keyword)
  `show_done`          true
  `source`             "agenda"
  `query`, `tag`       nil
  `sort`               "priority" | "deadline" | "scheduled" | "file"
  `layout`             "float" | "tab" | "split" | "vsplit" | "current"
  `width`, `height`    0.94, 0.88 (of the editor, or columns / lines)
  `min_column_width`   22
  `max_column_width`   44
  `title_lines`        2
  `card`               { priority, tags, deadline, effort = true,
                       scheduled, category = false }
  `save`               nil
  `keys`               the keys above, by name: `prev_state`, `next_state`,
                       `down`, `up`, `move_down`, `move_up`, `prev_column`,
                       `next_column`, `jump`, `refresh`, `filter`, `quit`

Highlight groups: `OrgKanbanTitle`, `OrgKanbanHint`, `OrgKanbanBorder`,
`OrgKanbanSelected`, `OrgKanbanCardTitle`, `OrgKanbanDoneTitle`,
`OrgKanbanCount`, `OrgKanbanWipExceeded`, `OrgKanbanOverdue`,
`OrgKanbanDueSoon`, `OrgKanbanDue`, `OrgKanbanScheduled`, `OrgKanbanEffort`,
`OrgKanbanCategory`, `OrgKanbanEmpty`.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Timeline                                             *org-extensions-timeline*

A text-mode Gantt chart: one row per task with a SCHEDULED or DEADLINE date,
on a date axis.
>lua
    require("org").setup({ extensions = { timeline = { zoom = "week" } } })
<
`:Org timeline [agenda|buffer|subtree|<file>] [day|week|month] [filter]`,
or the actions `timeline_open` (`<prefix>Vt`) and `timeline_buffer`.

A task's bar runs from its scheduled day to its deadline. With only one of
the two, the bar is as many days long as its Effort takes at
`hours_per_day` (8) working hours a day, starting on the scheduled day or
ending on the deadline. The deadline is drawn as ◆. A task past its deadline
is drawn in the error colour with a dashed trail up to today. Today's
column is shaded and its date highlighted on the axis; at day zoom
weekends are shaded too. With `clocks` (or `c`), the days a task was
clocked show as ▒. DONE tasks are left out unless `show_done` is set.

Times of day count at day zoom, where a day's three columns are its thirds
(0-8, 8-16, 16-24 h): `SCHEDULED: <... 14:00-15:00>` fills the middle one,
a bar starting at 14:00 starts there, and a deadline at 20:00 puts its ◆ in
the last one. A timed task with an Effort shorter than the rest of its day
ends when the Effort does. The week and month zooms show whole days.

A repeating task (`+1w`, `++1m`, `.+2d` on its SCHEDULED or DEADLINE) is
drawn again at each later occurrence inside the view, in
`OrgTimelineRepeat`: the whole bar and its deadline, shifted by the
repeat. Only occurrences from the current date on are drawn: the past of a
repeating task is its log, not its timestamp.

With the |org-extensions-ics| extension on and the agenda files as the
source, calendar events get a row each (a span per occurrence, in
`OrgTimelineEvent`), placed by their times like timed tasks; `ics = false`
leaves them out. They can't be opened or rescheduled from here.

In a narrow window the task names take at most 40% of the width.

Keys ~
  + = / -   zoom in / out: day (3 columns a day), week (a column a day),
            month (a column a week)
  [ / ]     pan half a screen to the past / future
  .         back to today
  <CR>      open the task
  S / D     reschedule / set the deadline of the task with the date prompt
            (as `C-c C-s` / `C-c C-d`)
  c         show or hide clocked days
  r         refresh
  q  <Esc>  quit

Options (defaults) ~
  `source`, `query`, `tag`   "agenda", nil, nil
  `show_done`          false
  `zoom`               "day"
  `days_before`        3 (days before today at the left edge)
  `label_width`        34
  `hours_per_day`      8
  `clocks`             false
  `ics`                true (calendar events, with the ics extension)
  `layout`             "float" (as for the kanban board)
  `width`, `height`    0.94, 0.88
  `save`               nil (save after S / D; nil follows
                       `agenda.save_after_edit`)
  `keys`               `zoom_in`, `zoom_out`, `pan_left`, `pan_right`,
                       `today`, `jump`, `schedule`, `deadline`, `clocks`,
                       `refresh`, `quit`

Highlight groups: `OrgTimelineTitle`, `OrgTimelineHint`, `OrgTimelineAxis`,
`OrgTimelineMonth`, `OrgTimelineSeparator`, `OrgTimelineBar`,
`OrgTimelineDone`, `OrgTimelineOverdue`, `OrgTimelineDeadline`,
`OrgTimelineClock`, `OrgTimelineToday`, `OrgTimelineTodayLabel`,
`OrgTimelineWeekend`, `OrgTimelineTask`, `OrgTimelineRepeat`,
`OrgTimelineEvent`.

Panning and zooming redraw without reading the files again; the timeline
is rebuilt when one of its files is written or changed (in Normal mode),
as the kanban board is.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Heatmap                                               *org-extensions-heatmap*

A calendar heatmap in the style of GitHub's contribution graph: a column per
week, a row per weekday, each day shaded by what was done that day.
>lua
    require("org").setup({ extensions = { heatmap = { weeks = 26 } } })
<
`:Org heatmap [clock|closed|habit] [tag|file]`, or the action
`heatmap_open` (`<prefix>Vh`). The kinds:
  `clock`   minutes clocked (CLOCK lines; a clock over midnight is split
          between the two days, a running clock counts up to now)
  `closed`  tasks marked done: CLOSED dates and `- State "DONE"` log
          entries, once per task and day
  `habit`   the same for habits (`STYLE: habit`)

A bare word is a tag (`+work` and `:work:` work too); a path ending in
`.org` is the source.

There are five shades: empty days, then four levels. By default the levels
split the days with any value in quartiles; `thresholds` sets the lower
bound of each level instead (e.g. `{ 30, 60, 120, 240 }` minutes). The
colours are mixed from the foreground of `color_group` and the background
of `Normal`, and follow colorscheme changes; `colors` sets all five.
Under the grid: the total, active days, the average of an active day, the
current streak (ending today, or yesterday while today is empty), the
longest one and the best day.

The cursor starts on today; the line at the bottom shows the selected day's
total and its biggest entries.

Keys ~
  <Tab>     next kind (clock, closed, habit)
  h / l     previous / next week
  k / j     previous / next day
  <CR>      the agenda of that day
  r         refresh
  q  <Esc>  quit

Options (defaults) ~
  `kind`          "clock"
  `source`, `tag` "agenda", nil
  `weeks`         0 (fit the window, at most 53)
  `cell`          "■"
  `thresholds`    nil
  `color_group`   "DiagnosticOk"
  `colors`        nil
  `layout`        "float"
  `keys`          `next_kind`, `prev_week`, `next_week`, `next_day`,
                  `prev_day`, `agenda`, `refresh`, `quit`

Highlight groups: `OrgHeatmap0` to `OrgHeatmap4` (the shades, set from the
options), `OrgHeatmapTitle`, `OrgHeatmapHint`, `OrgHeatmapLabel`,
`OrgHeatmapValue`, `OrgHeatmapDetail`.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Today sidebar                                         *org-extensions-sidebar*

A narrow window at the side of the tab that keeps today in view:
>lua
    require("org").setup({ extensions = { sidebar = { position = "left" } } })
<
The actions `sidebar_toggle` (`<prefix>Vs`), `sidebar_open` and
`sidebar_close`. Its sections (`sections`, in this order by default):
  `clock`   the running clock: the task, the time clocked and, with an
          Effort, a bar of the time against it (error colour once over)
  `next`    the next timed entry of today still to come, with a countdown
  `today`   today's timed entries (the past ones dimmed), deadlines due
          within `deadline_days` (the overdue ones first) and scheduled
          entries, from the agenda files, as in the day agenda
  `habits`  the habits due today
  `inbox`   the number of top-level entries not DONE in `inbox_file`
          (default: `default_notes_file`)

It is redrawn every `interval` seconds while it is open (the timer stops
when it closes) and when an org file is written or a clock or TODO state
changes. The window keeps its width (`winfixwidth`, and `winfixbuf` where
Neovim has it), has no numbers, sign column or status line text, and opening
it leaves the cursor where it was unless `focus` is set. Toggling it in
another tab moves it there.

Keys ~
  <CR>   open the entry (the clocked task, the inbox) in the window next
         to the sidebar
  r      refresh
  q      close

Options (defaults) ~
  `position`       "right"
  `width`          40
  `interval`       30
  `sections`       { "clock", "next", "today", "habits", "inbox" }
  `show_done`      false
  `deadline_days`  7
  `inbox_file`     nil
  `focus`          false
  `icons`          { clock = "◷", next = "»", today = "▣", habits = "↻",
                   inbox = "✉", deadline = "◆", scheduled = "▸" }
  `keys`           { jump = "<CR>", refresh = "r", close = "q" }

Highlight groups: `OrgSidebarTitle`, `OrgSidebarDate`, `OrgSidebarSection`,
`OrgSidebarCount`, `OrgSidebarTime`, `OrgSidebarPast`, `OrgSidebarText`,
`OrgSidebarEmpty`, `OrgSidebarCountdown`, `OrgSidebarOverdue`,
`OrgSidebarDue`, `OrgSidebarScheduled`, `OrgSidebarProgress`,
`OrgSidebarProgressEmpty`, `OrgSidebarOverrun`.

 vim:tw=78:ts=8:ft=help:norl:
